市場焦點
美國
• 9月CPI年增3.7%、PPI年增6.3%，雙雙高於前期 ，能源價格的急升是通膨回升的主要推力 。
雖然核心CPI年增率僅升至2.6%，但住房與服務價格仍維持黏著，使通膨降溫過程出現反覆；
同時PPI增幅明顯高於 CPI，反映企業成本壓力偏高 ，未來不排除進一步向終端價格傳導 。整
體而言，CPI與PPI同步回升反映通膨降溫進程仍存在不確定性 ，進而令市場重估通膨回落速
度與對Fed貨幣政策路徑的審慎預期。
AI
• AI雲端業者 CoreWeave的5年期CDS利差一度突破 1,140個基點 ，旗下兩檔債券殖利率逼近
15%，相關賣壓並進一步蔓延至股票市場 。全球最大半導體設備商ASML表示，客戶已開始洽
談2029至2030年的訂單；晶圓代工龍頭台積電財報亦優於市場預期 。與此同時，台灣出口增

速創3月以來新高，韓國9月半導體出口年增逾260%，顯示半導體需求持續強勁，預期第三季
相關企業獲利仍有望維持強勁。
中國
• 9月通膨加速主要源於中東能源衝擊及 AI硬體需求，能源和科技領域之外 ，普遍性的通縮壓力
仍在，彭博表示以GDP平減指數衡量 ，經歷三年下滑後 ，整體經濟預計將在 2026年走出通縮 ，
投資上短期可聚焦，受惠AI資本支出與能源價格上行的半導體、伺服器及能源相關族群。

美股獲利引擎轉向半導體/核心AI與能源
• 雖然標普500指數在2026年第三季展現出獲利韌性 ，但隨著油價上漲侵蝕利潤率 、消費者受通膨壓抑 ，以及分析師下調未
來預測動能，市場未來的容錯空間正在縮小。
• AI與半導體仍為獲利領頭羊：科技與半導體持續為標普500指數獲利成長的核心動力，其中約20家企業組成的核心AI一籃子
股票(權重約34.6%)對指數獲利的貢獻度已超過科技七雄(Mag 7)，且此優勢預計將至少延續至2028年底。
• 油價推升能源產業，接棒成為強棒：受到近期國際油價上揚影響，能源產業獲利預測顯著上修，預計獲利成長勁道將延續至
2027年初。在接下來的三個季度中，能源板塊的收益成長表現預計將超越科技七雄。
• 消費與運輸業面臨成本壓頂：相較於科技與能源的強勁，必需消費品與交通運輸業正承受顯著壓力。例如，達美航空受高燃

料成本拖累，而Nike與百事可樂則反映出消費者因持續通膨而縮減支出的困境。
7月以來EPS和營收成長預期修正
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.16
剔除能源、科技七雄和AI一籃子股票後的指數收益成長

利潤率高檔盤整、關稅退款帶來短補，但動能已見放緩
• 利潤率高檔盤整，淨利潤率提升受限：標普500成分股第二季營運利潤率(18.7%)與淨利潤率(15.6%)均創下新高。雖然第三
季營運利潤率預估可進一步提升至19.4%，但淨利潤率預計微幅上升至15.8%，顯示整體企業淨利潤的擴張動能正漸趨平緩 。
• 700億美元關稅退款提供短線補藥： 受惠於美國政府依據國際緊急經濟權力法 (IEEPA)的追償退稅，估計仍有至少700億美元
的關稅款將退還給標普 500成分股(主要集中於工業、非必需消費及醫療保健等 )，這預計能為2026年標普500指數每股收益
(EPS)成長帶來約235個基點、利潤率提升約34個基點的短期提振。
• 上修動能開始轉弱：雖然整體財報指引動量仍維持正值，但過去六週內，分析師對未來12個月EPS與收入的修正動能出現頻

繁下修，這意味著市場先前的極度樂觀情緒正在消退，未來財報季度要優於預期的門檻正在提高。
• 小結與投資建議： 標普500指數短期內靠著半導體 /AI與能源雙引擎拉抬 ，加上關稅退款的一次性利多注入 ，下半年整體獲
利依然具備支撐。然而，高油價帶來的營運成本上升 ，以及終端消費力道的疲軟 ，正逐漸侵蝕非科技/非能源企業的利潤 ，
而大盤廣度不足(僅少數板塊支撐獲利)，代表未來投資風險正逐漸集中 ，但AI算力需求仍屬硬性需求，但波動加劇下，投資
建議以定期定額方式投資美股核心AI與半導體龍頭股票。
利潤率預測
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.16
各產業第二季度收到的關稅退款
未來12月EPS預期修正動能與營收

顧
美國9月CPI、PPI 同步升溫，能源衝擊擴大通膨與利率風險
• 能源價格急升，消費通膨再升溫：美國9月CPI月增0.6%、
年增3.7%，高於前月 3.4%，其中汽油月增 9.1%、能源
價格月增 5.2%，汽油單項即貢獻整體 CPI當月漲幅逾半；
另一方面，核心CPI月增0.3%、年增2.6%、住房指數月
增0.4%，顯示能源雖是本次通膨回升主因 ，但住房及部
分服務價格仍具黏著性，令通膨降溫進程面臨反覆。
• 上游價格大幅攀升 ，成本傳導風險升高： 9月PPI最終需
求月增1.3%、年增6.3%，創2022年3月以來最大增幅，
其中最終需求商品月增 2.6%，能源價格大漲 10.8%，汽
油價格更跳升 20.3%；核心PPI月增0.5%、年增5.0%，
顯示價格壓力不僅來自能源 ，也涉及部分商品與服務環

節；後續是否進一步推升至 CPI，仍須觀察企業成本轉
嫁程度及需求變化。
• 市場看法： 通膨回升恐提高 Fed再度升息機率 ，並延長
高利率維持時間 ，甚至不排除進一步緊縮的可能 ，短期
有利支撐美債殖利率及美元表現 ，不過股、債市後續走
勢仍將受到貨幣政策預期與風險情緒變化影響 。資產配
置建議可關注具成本轉嫁能力 、現金流穩健的能源類股；
債券則宜以短中天期 、高信用評等標的為主 ，以降低利
率敏感度。黃金可作為分散地緣政治及通膨風險的輔助
配置，但不宜將任何單一資產視為必然受惠標的。
CPI 與核心 CPI 走勢變動

顧
供給面推動中國9月CPI升0.9%
• 供給面推動中國9月通膨復甦：中國9月通膨數據顯示，CPI年增率由8月的0.8%升至0.9%，連續兩個月擴大，但低於市場預期
的1.0%，物價上漲主要受到能源及旅遊價格帶動，而食品價格跌幅進一步擴大，使整體通膨壓力仍相對溫和，扣除食品與能源
後，核心CPI維持在1.0%，反映內需需求尚未明顯升溫 。另一方面，PPI年增率由3.8%大幅升至5.0%，優於市場預期，且月增
率達1.2%，創4月以來最大漲幅，顯示上游原物料及工業品價格回升，須關注未來是否逐步傳導至消費端價格。
• 展望後市： 9月通膨加速主要源於中東能源衝擊及 AI硬體需求 ，能源和科技領域之外 ，普遍性的通縮壓力仍 在，彭博表示 以
GDP平減指數衡量，經歷三年下滑後，整體經濟預計將在2026年走出通縮，值得注意的是，這種供給面驅動的通膨復甦，將降

低官方推出需求刺激措施的緊迫感 ，而要反轉通縮循環正需要官方舉措，投資上短期可聚焦受惠AI資本支出與能源價格上行的
半導體、伺服器及能源相關族群。
資料來源：彭博，2026.10.14
通膨集中於能源和AI相關行業

顧
過去18個月來，新興市場股票表現亮眼，累積報酬率超過標普500指數
的兩倍
• 過去18個月來，新興市場股票表現亮眼，累積報酬率超過標普500指數的兩倍：主因為獲利帶動成長，整體而言，新興市場企
業獲利表現強勁 ，而且仍持續加速成長 ，高盛預估2026年新興市場企業盈餘成長率將達 55%，超過標普500企業盈餘成長速
度的兩倍，且新興市場股票估值較美國股市存在超過40%的折價空間，評價相對具吸引力，今年以來流入新興市場股市的資金
已接近500億美元，創下過去20年來最強勁紀錄之一。更重要的是新興市場國家目前貢獻了全球近 80%的AI硬體出口。隨著AI
產業持續發展，受惠範圍有望從少數龍頭企業擴大至更多國家及公司，特別是那些能夠協助解決供應鏈瓶頸的企業。
• 結論：後市來看在AI產業趨勢、相對低估的評價水準，以及強勁企業獲利成長三大因素支撐下，新興市場股票未來一年仍具備

進一步上漲的潛力，有機會延續目前的強勢表現。
MSCI新興市場股票表現亮眼

AI投資支撐美國成長，融資需求升高使長端利率與信用風險並存
• AI基礎建設持續擴張 ，成為企業投資重要動能 ：大型雲端業者
持續投入資料中心 、半導體、網路設備與電力基礎設施 ，帶動
相關產業資本支出與供應鏈需求 。摩根大通資產管理指出 ，納
入資料中心開發商及半導體投資後，AI 基礎建設至 2030 年的
累計投資需求可能達約 5兆美元 ，顯示AI投資循環仍具規模 ，
但實際經濟貢獻與企業獲利 ，仍取決於投資效率及商業化成果 。
• 外部融資需求增加 ，債券供給與投資回報成關鍵： 隨AI投資規
模擴大，大型科技企業逐步增加公司債與外部融資 ，在企業債
與公債供給同步增加下 ，長天期利率面臨上行壓力 。若未來AI
獲利成長未能跟上資本支出擴張速度 ，可能壓縮自由現金流與

資本報酬率，進一步擴大信用利差 ，並提高高估值科技股的評
價修正風險
• 市場觀察：AI基礎建設仍是未來數年推動全球科技投資與企業
數位轉型的成長主軸，相關需求具備中長期發展空間，而2026
年市場已逐漸轉向檢視投資回報率 、現金流創造能力及融資結
構是否具備可持續性 ，因此單純依賴資本支出擴張已不足以支
撐所有AI概念股評價 。建議配置可兼顧具獲利與現金流支撐的
AI供應鏈，以及高信用評等 、中短天期債券；對長天期債券則
須評估供給與利率風險 ，並審慎檢視高負債 AI企業的償債能力
及投資回報。
資料來源：Goldman Sachs，永豐金證券， 2026.10.12

AI熱潮、高債務與網路威脅，IMF示警三大金融穩定風險升高
資料來源：Bloomberg Finance L.P， Dealogic，Zero day Clock and IMF staff calculations.， 2026.10.13
AI相關風險持續升溫 主權債券殖利率攀升 網路資安威脅升高
大型雲端科技企業債券發行規模
（占投資等級公司債市場比重) 10年期公債殖利率平均變動幅度 零時差漏洞占比（%）
• AI估值高度集中，市場重定價風險升溫：IMF於10月《全球金融穩定報告》指出，AI相關投資已成為全球成長的重要支撐，
但資金與漲勢高度集中於少數企業及市場，一旦獲利成長不如預期或估值遭重新檢視，可能引發科技股修正並透過風險偏好
下降擴散至整體金融市場。
• 通膨與財政疑慮恐推升債券市場波動： IMF認為，若能源價格推升通膨壓力或市場對財政永續性產生疑慮 ，主權債殖利率可

能進一步上升，進而提高全球融資成本與資產重估風險；在高利率與高債務環境下，金融條件快速緊縮的情況將明顯提高。
• 網路安全風險成為潛在威脅： 隨金融體系數位化程度提高 ，IMF將大型網路攻擊列為重要風險來源之一 。若攻擊事件波及支
付系統、金融基礎設施或雲端服務平台，不僅可能影響市場運作效率，也可能透過信心衝擊與流動性壓力擴大金融市場波動。
• 投資建議：市場成長動能仍受AI支撐，但在金融穩定風險上升情境下，建議採取「AI受惠股＋高品質債券＋防禦型資產」的
槓鈴策略；核心配置可聚焦全球AI基礎建設供應鏈及投資等級債，藉由企業長期獲利成長與債券避險功能，同時因應估值修
正及金融條件收緊風險。

AI隱憂浮現但動能仍在
• CoreWeave信用風險亮紅燈：市場憂心雲端業者在算力租賃業務產生足夠回報前，恐難以負擔龐大債務，導致信用風險升
溫。上週，AI雲端業者CoreWeave的5年期信用違約交換（CDS）利差一度突破1,140個基點，升至通常反映嚴重信用壓力
的水準；旗下兩檔債券殖利率逼近15%，其中2032年到期的無擔保債券於上週三跌破面值的80%，相關賣壓並進一步蔓延
至股票市場。
• AI半導體產業的基本面仍具韌性：全球最大半導體設備商艾司摩爾（ASML）表示，客戶已開始洽談2029至2030年的訂單；
晶圓代工龍頭台積電財報亦優於市場預期。與此同時，台灣出口增速創3月以來新高，韓國9月半導體出口年增逾260%，顯
示半導體需求持續強勁，預期第三季相關企業獲利仍有望維持強勁。

美銀美林調查指出，全球經理人對景氣與股市仍維持偏多立場
資料來源：BofA Global Fund Manager Survey，2026.10
• 經理人樂觀情緒維持高檔： 美銀美林10月全球基金經理人調查顯示 ，市場風險偏好仍高 ，平均現金水位維持在 3.9%的低檔，
科技股配置則升至2020年8月以來最高，反映經理人對AI產業及企業獲利前景仍具信心 。然而，整體經濟成長預期已轉趨保
守，淨4%的受訪者預期未來 12個月經濟成長放緩 。極端風險方面，債券殖利率無序攀升仍居首位 ，占比31%；AI泡沫及通
膨再起分別為23%及20%。此外，39%的經理人認為，大型雲端業者資本支出是系統性信用事件的主要潛在來源 ，顯示市場
焦點已從AI需求成長延伸至龐大資本支出的獲利回收能力。

• 美國公債殖利率走高 ，債券的收益吸引力逐漸回升： 調查顯示，37%的受訪者表示，當美國10年期公債殖利率升至 6%時，
才會考慮由股票轉向債券；另有19%認為目前殖利率已具吸引力，顯示部分經理人已開始重新評估固定收益資產的配置價值 。
不過，債券整體仍處淨減碼，且經理人對科技股的配置持續升高 ，反映市場尚未全面轉向防禦。後續仍須關注美債殖利率變
化、AI資本支出的投資回報，以及企業獲利能否支撐偏高的市場評價。
資料來源：BofA Global Fund Manager Survey，2026.10

台積電財報佳卻未激勵股價，短線面臨總經干擾
• 台積電Q3營收、獲利及EPS同步改寫單季新高：營收年增50.9%、季增17.6%，稅後純益7,630.6億元，年增68.7％、季增8
％；每股稅後純益(EPS)29.43元，整體優於市場預期，但毛利率66.4％較第二季的67.7％下降。
• 財測展望：第四季財測營收預估 523~543億美元，季增上看15％、年增65％，毛利率預估65%~67%，反映高階晶片需求
依然強勁。全年展望方面，預期2026年美元營收年增約44%，較上季預估的「略高於40%」說法更加明確。此外，重申全
年資本支出600~640億美元不變。
• 市場反應：台積電法說會隔日股價小幅下跌，法說會未出現激勵行情，主要原因包含美國通膨升溫壓力、外資連續調節部位，

以及毛利率略為下降。短線籌碼面可能仍會調整，但台積電董事長魏哲家在法說會中強調，第二波AI需求正強勁興起，台積
電正透過多種途徑全力擴充產能，顯示高階晶片前景明確，中長期具投資價值，多家投資機構也紛紛上調目標價。
資料來源：台積電法說會，兆豐銀行整理，2026.10.15。
法說會重點整理 說明
AI需求持續升溫
魏哲家坦言，目前仍難以判斷晶圓供需缺口將擴大或縮小，因為
客戶需求持續增加，台積電也正積極提高產能。儘管消費性電子
市場仍受到零組件漲價及總體經濟不確定性影響，但先進製程需
求依然強勁，公司預估2026年全年美元營收將年增約44％。
擴廠進度
(1)台南科學園區新建3奈米廠預計2027年下半年量產，並決定在
台南再增建一座3奈米廠，規畫2028年投入量產。
(2)美國亞利桑那州第二座晶圓廠已完成廠房興建，預計2027年
第三季量產，時程較原先規畫略為提前；日本第二座晶圓廠則將
導入3奈米製程，預計2028年量產。
台積電證實洽談Terafab
合作
魏哲家強調，目前相關合作仍在討論階段，尚未有結論。不過，

無論未來採取何種合作模式，晶圓廠都必須由台積電負責營運。
電力供應疑慮
美國電力供應情況是公司評估AI長期需求的重要因素之一，因此
台積電進一步確認當地電力供應狀況，目前相關研究及評估結果
均良好，尚未發現明顯問題。

• 全球經濟正處於「韌性仍在、風險升高」的階段：國際貨幣基金組織(IMF)指出，AI投資熱潮持續推動美國、台灣等國家成
長，但中東地緣政治衝突、高通膨風險、政府債務問題與AI泡沫化疑慮，也同時為市場埋下不確定因素。
• 市場永遠充滿雜音，定期定額紀律投資更具優勢：本許多人認為定期定額的核心在於「平均成本」，但更重要的是幫助投資
人克服人性。市場上漲時持續投入，可以參與企業獲利成長帶來的成果，市場下跌時持續扣款，則能以較低價格累積更多單
位數。換句話說，定期定額讓投資人不需要每天判斷：明天會不會跌？現在是不是太貴？要不要先停扣觀望？長期而言，紀

律投資往往比短期擇時的成效更佳。
• AI時代更適合定期定額：儘管IMF認為人工智慧發展為全球經濟成長帶來貢獻，但同時也警告，若市場對AI未來獲利能力過
度樂觀，不排除出現股市估值修正。根據歷史經驗，每一次市場重大修正往往也孕育下一輪成長契機，不論AI發展是否出現
泡沫，善用定期定額策略可以在機會與風險之間達到平衡。
韌性仍在、風險升溫，紀律投資更穩健
任一月份定期定額進場投資試算
註：以MSCI全球指數2010/1/31~2025/12/31試算。假設定期定額每月月底投入、
原幣計算。指數資料試算僅供參考，非投資商品之實際績效。資料來源Bloomberg。
短進短出錯失行情的代價大
註：以美國標普500指數為試算範例，期間為1980年~2025年。資料來源Bloomberg。

前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊 ，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣 (DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險 。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。
11. 屬基金資訊觀測站ESG基金專區所列之基金，有關基金之ESG資訊，投資人應於申購前詳閱基金公開說明書或投資人須知所載之基金所有特色或目標等資訊。

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We provide our latest views on Delta after its 3Q26 results 
call on 29 October.

What’s the impact: Business outlook. During the results call, Delta 
remained positive on AI monetisation after its analysis of Anthropic’s S-1 
filing. Management flagged shortages of components, power and skilled 
electricians and slower-than-expected data centre buildout to be potential 
hiccups in the process; however, it still sees strong underlying demand. As 
such, we still expect sequential revenue growth in both 4Q26E and 1Q27E 
as component shortages had deferred 3Q26 demand. On rising material 
costs, Delta noted that BoM costs have increased by 2% as of 3Q26 (vs. 
early 2026), with the company gradually passing these costs through to 
end customers in 4Q26 and 1Q27. We believe the main gross margin 
driver will likely still be the absence of inventory write-offs and a higher 
power mix in 4Q26E. We forecast a gross margin recovery to 36% and a 
record operating margin of 18.5% in 4Q26. As for long-term growth, we 
maintain our investment theses that DC-DC module and HVDC adoption 
will become key growth drivers for Delta in 2027 and 2028, respectively.

3Q26 results missed on gross margin. Delta's 3Q26 earnings were 
TWD23.5bn (EPS: TWD9.04), missing our estimate of TWD27.3bn and 
Bloomberg consensus estimate of TWD29.6bn by 14% and 21%, 
respectively. We attribute the weakness mainly to lower-than-expected 
gross margin, driven by: 1) higher-than-expected inventory write-offs amid 
product shipment hiccups; 2) a one-off provision for customer claims, with 
the related loss likely to ultimately be borne by the supplier; and 3) rising 
material costs. Based on our calculation, Delta recognised c.TWD4bn of 
inventory provision loss in 3Q26, which reduced gross margin by 2pp. 
Excluding inventory provision loss, gross margin was c.35.8%, close to our 
previous estimate of 36.3%. 3Q26 revenue reached TWD197.1bn (up 8% 
QoQ and 31% YoY), which was also negatively affected by product 
shipment hiccups (vs. our previous estimate of 10% QoQ growth).

What we recommend: We trim our 2026-28E EPS by 2-6% to reflect lower 
revenue assumptions due to the impact of component shortages. We 
reaffirm our Buy (1) call but trim our 12M TP to TWD3,000 (from 
TWD3,088) based on an unchanged target PER of 49x (high-end of its 3-
year range of 11-50x) applied to our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. Key 
downside risk: lower-than-expected demand.

How we differ: Our 2028E revenue is 12% above the Bloomberg 
consensus, likely on our more bullish assumption regarding HVDC 
adoption.

29 October 2026

3Q26 review: margin likely bottomed-out

➢ Gross margin to recover on lower inventory provisions in 4Q26 
➢ Addressable market remains intact despite some near-term hiccups

Company profile 
Founded in 1971, Delta Electronics has been the global leader in switching power supply solutions since 
2002 and DC brushless fans since 2006. Its business covers 3 key areas: power electronics (eg, power 
supplies, thermal management products and passive components), automation (eg, controllers, robot 
arms and building management systems), and infrastructure (eg, datacentre infrastructure, networking 
and EV charging).

### Page 5

5

Delta Electronics (2308 TT): 29 October 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 30 Sep 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
Delta's board has 7 non-independent and 5 independent directors with a term of three years.
The total number of directors and ratio of independent directors appear higher than most other
listed companies. Besides, according to Delta's CSR report, enhancing the board's functions is
one of the key goals for the company to achieve better corporate governance; also, the board
members' diversity and professionalism will be taken into consideration to ensure a good board
quality. Moreover, the company evaluates all the directors in the board using internal regulations
and an external independent institution each year and at least once every three years,
respectively. We believe Delta has implemented relatively more enhanced measure than most
listed firms to improve its board quality.
Capital management 2
During 2016-19, Delta's payout ratio was stable in the range of c.70%, but fell to 56% in 2020
and 53% in 2021. For 2020, it was mainly due to a sizeable non-cash re-valuation gain after the
acquisition of Delta Thailand (DET), while the company maintained more cash on hand to deal
with the uncertainties caused by COVID-19. However, the DPS growth from 2016 to 2021 was
limited, as Delta struggled with business diversification into new end-markets (eg, smartphone,
NEV). We believe that Delta's effort started to bear fruit in recent years after its NEV business
has gradually picked up. Overall, we see a stable dividend policy from Delta and expect its DPS
to continue to grow YoY in the future given that they declared a TWD11.6 DPS for 2025 which
equals a 50% payout ratio.
Related party &
transaction 1 Revenue from related parties stood at 12% of total 2025 revenue and purchases from related parties were at 11%. We see limited risk from related party transactions.
S Supply chain management 1
Delta focuses on: 1) localised management, 2) supplier certification and risk management, and
3) supplier sustainability engagement as key initiatives under the scope of supply chain
management. In 2019, 144 new suppliers joined Delta's supply chain system. All of them passed
environmental and social evaluations, signed the declaration of "non-use of conflict materials",
and complied with Responsible Business Alliance's (BRA) Code of Conduct. Also, these vendors
had to implement and abide by Delta's supply chain CSR policy and code of conduct. To lower
ESG risks, Delta carries out RBA audits and monitors high-risk critical suppliers each year. The
suppliers that fail these audits have to provide detailed improvement plans in response to
identified issues. Besides, Delta has surveyed a total of 263 suppliers with potential risk and 63
suppliers with high risk of the total 1,331 suppliers, based on its CSR policy and code of conduct.
If these suppliers are flagged as high-risk and no concrete improvements are made, Delta may
consider terminating cooperation and transactions with them to improve the company's
engagement on supplier sustainability. Overall, we think Delta has a solid plan to improve its
supply chain management with specific KPIs, including: 1) achieving supplier improvement rate
of 80% in 2025, 2) reducing GHG emissions rate of critical suppliers by 10% in 2025, and 3)
improving water saving rate of critical suppliers by 12% in 2025.
S Data security 2
Delta's Information Security team created Delta's Information Security Policy in November 2013
and started providing educational training to global employees in 2014. Delta gradually
introduced the Information Security Management System (ISMS) in 2016 and obtained the ISO
27001 international certification in 2018. According to ISO27001, Delta has to conduct at least
one internal audit on information security and appoint an independent institution to conduct audit
externally. According to Delta, no major deficiencies have been found in recent years.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

Greater China Technology Semiconductors
Asia Pacific
Industry View Attractive
2027 capex growth is highlighted: The Big Four hyperscalers' combined C3Q capex
was US$185bn, +90% Y/Y. CY2026 guidance stands at approximately US$170bn for
Microsoft, US$195–205bn for Google, US$135–145bn for Meta, and US$220bn for
Amazon. Google and Amazon maintained guidance; Meta raised its spending floor

US$5bn. More importantly, Microsoft expects spending to accelerate in January–
June 2027, Google anticipates a significant increase in 2027, and Amazon expects
meaningfully higher spending. Meta also plans to keep investing aggressively.
Supply constraints remain the issue: Microsoft reported C3Q26 capex of US
$45.2bn and expects C4Q spending >US$50bn, with some capacity deliveries
shifting into 1H27. Google invested US$51.4bn and turned to third-party computing

late in C3Q to address supply constraints, with usage rising in C4Q. Meta invested
US$34.9bn, including finance lease principal payments; Amazon’s cash capex
reached US$75.9bn. Inference already contributes well over half of AWS AI revenue.
Debating monetization: Microsoft generated US$18.6bn of quarterly FCF and
expects positive FCF in FY2027. Google, Meta, and Amazon reported negative
quarterly FCF of US$9.1bn, US$2.1bn, and US$33.5bn, respectively. Google also

flagged depreciation, data center operating costs, and third-party computing
expenses as near-term cloud margin headwinds, alongside TPU inventory cash
requirements. Management remains focused on attractive long-term returns.
Cloud semis: Constructive read-through. Sustained server investment supports
Aspeed's and Montage's demand outlook; delayed capacity could shift revenue
between quarters, but it's just a timing issue. Approximately 75% of Microsoft’s C3Q

capex targeted short-lived assets, including CPUs, GPUs, and higher memory/storage
costs, up from two-thirds mentioned before. Google allocated ~65% to servers and
networking.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of
interest that could affect the objectivity of Morgan Stanley
Research. Investors should consider Morgan Stanley

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
2027 capex is highlighted: Microsoft, Google and Amazon flag higher spending;
Meta remains committed to aggressive investment.
Capacity constraints, not budget: Microsoft’s delivery delays and Google’s third-
party computing usage point to infrastructure bottlenecks.
Cloud semis: Constructive read-through so far. Sustained hyperscaler spending

provides a favorable backdrop, with capacity additions raising demand.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

### Page 1

M
Idea
Greater China  Semiconductors  | Asia Pacific
PC Semis: More divergent
outlooks
Business diversification, KGD (known good die) pricing power
and supply chain management create a divergent revenue
outlook for PC semis into 4Q and likely also 1Q27. Downgrade
Elan to EW.
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+
Daniel Yen, CFA
Equity Analyst
Daniel.Yen@morganstanley.com                                                +886 2 2730-2863
Charlie Chan
Equity Analyst

Morgan Stanley appreciates your support in
the 2027 Extel Asia Research Survey. Voting
opens 10 November 2026.
Greater China Technology Semiconductors
Asia Pacific
Industry View Attractive
What’s Changed
Elan Microelectronics Corp
(2458.TW) From To
Rating Overweight Equal-weight
Price Target NT$250.00 NT$160.00
A much tougher 4Q… We mentioned in the prior PC semis note that the FOMP
(fear of missing procurement) could lead to  stable PC semis shipments in 3Q; now,

the inventory accumulation in 1Q-3Q26 could cause a deeper correction in 4Q.  PC
semis customers currently still aim for only a 5-10% Q/Q procurement decline in 4Q.
That said, the downside surprise in Parade’s 4Q outlook (link), which calls for a 20-
25% Q/Q revenue decline, could suggest a worse component correction. The still-
elevated Windows activation period of 200+ days and much smaller price hikes
from PC semis customers in 2H vs. 1H could suggest ongoing weakness into 1Q27.

But why are some companies resilient? Many investors are asking why Realtek’s
revenue could be up Q/Q in 4Q amid the PC correction (link). We cite three factors:
1) Consumer demand (other than PC/smartphones) is not necessarily all bad,
especially for IoT devices (low memory content). Espressif is guiding for  Q/Q
revenue growth in 4Q, following sequential growth in 3Q. 2) Price hikes reflect
increases at both  foundries/OSATs and in KGD. For example, TV SoC pricing is

several times higher than in past years due to  KGD price hikes (mainly DDR4). 3)
Large-scale IC design houses usually have better supply chain management and
lower exposure to PC.
No new growth driver into 2027: With the recent debate on cloud AI, we think it’s
still a matter of time before edge AI  takes off. However, we haven’t seen a clear
growth driver for PC semis. Preliminary outlooks from PC semis customers range
from flattish to down 20% Y/Y in 2027. Thus, it's  difficult to turn positive now.

Reduce earnings: We revise down Elan's PT and earnings and cut the stock to EW.
We appreciate the company's growth from smart Edge AI devices, but its exposure
to NB is still too significant to overlook. Our PT implies 16x our downward-revised
2027 EPS. Stay EW  Realtek and UW  Novatek, ASMedia, Parade, and Nuvoton.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
The FOMP (fear of missing procurement for PC semis in 1Q-3Q26) has caused a
deeper correction for some PC semis into 4Q.
It's easier for large-scale PC semis  to navigate through the downturn, while small
design houses are more concentrated on PC.
We stay conservative on PC semis, with no OW and await the cycle bottom

sometime in 2027.
November 3, 2026 09:00 PM GMT

Idea
2
Elan Micro: Earnings estimate revisions
We revise our EPS estimates by +1%/-20%/-16% for 2026/27/28: For 2026, we
incorporate actual 2Q26 results, which benefited from investment gains. For 2027-28, we
reduce our revenue and GM forecasts due to continued weak PC demand, which we
believe cannot be fully offset by the non PC business (mainly the drone market).
Exhibit 1: Elan: Estimate revisions
Source: Company data, Morgan Stanley Research (e) estimates.

Exhibit 2: Elan: Quarterly financial statement
Source: Company data, Morgan Stanley Research (e) estimates
Valuation Methodology
We cut our price target to NT$160: This reflects our earnings estimate revisions for
2026-28. Our price target is our base-case scenario value, which we continue to derive
from our residual income model. We reduce our intermediate growth rate estimate from
10.0% to 8.0% to reflect a weak PC business outlook. We reduce our terminal growth rate

estimate from 4.5% to 3.0% to better align it with broader economic growth. We continue
to assume a cost of equity of 9.6% and a payout ratio of 80%.
Our bull case value is cut to NT$234 from NT$366, and our bear case value is cut to NT
$84 from NT$131.

Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

群聯(8299)法說會前財報預估 
受惠 AI 帶動儲存需求，4Q26 營運動能延續

| 財報訊息   
群聯即將於 11/6(五)舉行法說會， 3Q26營收季增 26.79%至860.7億
元， 優於預期 ，市場普遍預估毛利率介於56~65%，EPS約124.22元，
關注公司對於2027年NAND市況、產業供需看法以及AI生態系佈局
與應用。 
| 營運現況  
3Q26營收季增26.79%，優於預期：3Q26營收季增26.79%至860.7億
元，優於預期，主要反映AI基礎建設、AI推論及企業級儲存需求快
速成長，2Q26公司AI Ecosystem modules 占比38%，然而9月公司AI 
Ecosystem modules 營收貢獻度已超過整體營收50%，考量在NAND
成本推升下，推估毛利率64.5%，稅後EPS 134.52元。 
| 營運展望  
預估4Q26營收季增 11.92%：預估 4Q26營收季增 11.92%至963.3億
元，動能來自持續受惠AI帶動儲存、運算、網路及相關基礎建設需
求，9月公司AI Ecosystem modules包括 eSSD 、aiDAPTIV solution、
AI PC、AI Networking、AI Server及Boot Drive開機碟等AI相關應用，
營收貢獻度已超過整體營收50%，反映公司Phison 3.0策略轉型，優
化產品結構 ， 目前公司近9成在於高附加價值市場應用 ， 跳脫以往傳
統零售業務景氣波動影響。先前法說公司提及目前 NAND供應短缺
仍是最大限制，預期2H26價格仍維持漲勢，考量隨著購入NAND成
本墊高下，推估毛利率62.4%，稅後EPS 142.08元。展望2027年，隨
著AI推理從文本、圖像、影片走向AI Agent產生TB級資料量，將推
升高容量、高效能儲存需求，將支撐公司後續營運動能。

주가 추이

3Q26 매출액과 영 업 이 익 은 모두 당사 추정치를 크게 하회했습니다. 탁월
한 차량 판매 증가세에도 불구하고 부정적 믹스와 환율/원자재가 상승에 따
른 수익성 악화가 아 쉬 웠 습 니 다. 매크로 영향 고려한 추 정 치 하향 및 로봇 
지분가치 할인으로 목표주가 21만원으로 조정합니다. 
큰 폭의 판매 성장에도 수익성 크게 악화 
동사 3Q26 매출액은 31.6조원(+10.1% 이하 YoY), 영업이익 1.8조원
(+24.3%)을 기록하며 추정치를 크게 하회했다. 관세 영향이 본격화되었
던 전년 동기 대비 관세율 차이에 따른 비용 감소(5.3천억원)와 판매보증 
충당부채 전입액 감소(6.7천억원) 등 낮은 기저로 이익이 개선되었으나, 
2023년 생산 정상화 이후 가장 큰 폭의 판매 성장(84.7만대, +7.9%)에도 
불구하고 인센티브와 재료비 상승, 중동 물량 감소와 경제형 EV 판매 확
대에 따른 부정적 믹스로 OPM 5.8%에 그친 실적을 기록했다.  
예상보다 큰 부정적 PT/지역 믹스 영향 
기대를 상회하는 판매량 증가에 따라 물량 효과 통한 수익성 개선을 기
대했으나, 예상 대비 큰 3Q 부정적 믹스 영향에 따라 4Q와 ’27년 실적 
개선 기대 또한 낮출 수밖에 없는 상황이다. 아중동 판매 부진과 유럽 모
델 수익성 저하 영향이 판매 물량 증가 효과보다 크게 작용하고 있다. 인
도, 중남미 대비 고마진 차종 믹스가 좋은 중동의 지정학적 영향이 지속
되고 있고, 유럽은 EV2 등 신차 중심 판매 확대에도 중국 로컬업체들의 
유럽 공세 격화로 시장 점유율을 사수하기 위한 모델 저수익화가 예상 
대비 장기화될 것으로 판단한다. 그러나 고정비 만회를 위해 인센티브 
증가와 가격 효과 축소 영향 속에서도 판매 확대는 불가피한 상황이다. 
부정적 상황 장기화 가능성, 목표주가 210,000원으로 하향 
결국, 수익성 개선을 위해서는 고마진 제품 판매 확대 또는 판매가 견조
하나 저마진 차종 원가를 절감하는 방법밖에 없다. 북미 텔루라이드 
HEV 판매와 스포티지HEV HMGMA 증산이 고마진 물량 증가에 지속
기여할 것이다. 그러나 단기 중동 시장 회복과 차종별 원가절감 효과 확
인이 어렵다는 점에서 고마진 제품 판매 확대 효과는 제한적 전망이다. 
이에, 투자의견 Buy 유지하되, 목표주가 21만원으로 조정한다. 실적 추
정치 조정하여 영업가치를 종전 대비 15.7% 하향하고, 성장주 밸류 디스
카운트에 따른 로봇 지분가치 30% 할인하였다.

[ Compliance Notice ] (공표일: 2026 년 11 월 2 일) 
이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 7

기아 (000270) [한화리서치]

7 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

[ 당사 조사분석자료의 투자등급 부여 비중 ] (기준일: 2026 년 9 월 30 일) 
투자등급 매수 중립 매도 합계 
금융투자상품의 비중 96.4% 3.6% 0.0% 100.0%

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
26년 3분기, 물량 감소에도 확인된 이익 방어력 
현대차의 3Q26 매출액은 45조 1,100억원(Y oY -3.4%), 영업이익은 2조 6,770억원
(Y oY +5.5%), OPM 5.9%(Y oY +0.5%p)를 기록했다. 국내 파업 및 영업일수 감소 영
향으로 글로벌 도매 판매량은 92.1만대(Y oY -11.3%)까지 감소했으나, HEV 중심의 
고부가 차종 Mix 개선, 기말환율 하락에 따른 판매보증비(Y oY -1.2조원) 감소 효과
로 수익성은 오히려 개선됐다. 특히 HEV 판매 비중은 18.2%(Y oY +2.7%p)까지 상
승했다. 반면 EV 판매는 전년 대비 감소세가 이어지며 지역별 수요 둔화와 경쟁 심
화 영향이 지속됐다. 
연간 눈높이는 낮아졌지만, 믹스 개선은 지속 
현대차는 글로벌 수요 둔화와 생산 차질을 반영해 2026년 글로벌 도매 판매 목표를 
기존 415.8만대에서 400만대로, 영업이익률 가이던스를 기존 6.3~7.3%에서 
5.5~6.5%로 하향 조정했다. 다만 북미를 중심으로 HEV 판매가 견조하게 유지되고 
있으며, 하반기 주요 신차 투입과 전동화 라인업 확대를 통해 믹스 개선 기조는 이
어질 전망이다. 중장기적으로는 로보택시 공급 확대와 Atlas 상용화 등 Physical AI 
사업도 계획대로 진행 중으로, 단기 판매 성장 둔화에도 자동차 본업의 수익성 방어
와 신사업 모멘텀은 유효한 것으로 판단한다. 
투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 460,000원 하향 
글로벌 완성차 업체들의 수익성 부담은 확대되고 있다. 3분기 자동차 부문 영업이익
률은 Mercedes-Benz 2.5%, Stellantis 0.7%, Volvo -5.6%, VW -7.8%를 기록했다. 
유럽 내 BEV 판매 확대에도 중국 전기차 업체들의 공격적인 해외 진출과 가격 경
쟁, 중국 시장 부진이 동시에 나타나면서 전통 OEM의 수익성 압박이 심화되고 있
다. 이러한 환경에서 현대차는 HEV 중심의 유연한 파워트레인 전략과 북미 중심의 
Mix 개선을 통해 5% 후반의 수익성을 유지하고 있다는 점이 상대적으로 부각된다.

Source PDF: all report pdf/[012450] 한화에어로스페이스(012450) 일시적 부진, 4Q부터 성장 가도에 재진입한화에어로스페이스(012450) 일시적 부진, 4Q부터 성장 가도에_b94e5eb0.pdf

### Page 1

한화에어로스페이스 (012450/KS) 
일시적 부진, 4Q부터 성장 가도에 재진입 
2026-10-30

조선/방산. 한승한, RA. 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 1,300,000 원(하향) 
현재주가: 760,000 원 
상승여력: 71.1%

Signal: 3Q26, 수출 물량 저조 및 국내 낮은 마진 물량 인식으로 어닝 쇼크 
Key: 4Q부터 내년까지 수출 물량 빠르게 회복하며 실적 성장 이어갈 전망 
Step: 연내 새로운 중장기 성장동력이 될 중동 수주계약 기대. 매수 기회

3Q26 Review: 수출 물량 납품 저조로 어닝 쇼크  
3Q26 연결 매출액 6 조 943 억원(-6.0% YoY, -34.4% QoQ), 영업이익 4,520억원
(-46.2% YoY, -66.9% QoQ)으로, 시장예상치(1조 1,615억원)를 대폭 하회하는 어닝 쇼
크 기록했다. ①폴란드 중심의 수출 물량의 매출 인식 시점이 4 분기로 이연되며, 수출 물량 
비중이 감소한 점이 컸다. ②내수 물량 또한 감소했으며, 특히 국내 물량 중에서도 상대적
으로 마진율 낮은 유무인 무기체계 초도 물량이 이번 분기에 집중됐다. ③내수&수출 전반
적인 지상방산 매출 비중 감소에 따른 고정비 부담이 컸으며, ④일회성 요인으로 대전 사업
장 사고에 따른 복구 비용 약 300 억원과 충당금 및 기타 비용 총 160 억원, 그리고 환율 
하락으로 영업이익의 약 10% 수준의 감소 효과 발생했다. 다만 4Q26 부터 이번 분기 이
연된 폴란드 중심의 수출 물량 인식이 반영되면서 큰 폭의 탑라인 및 이익 성장 시현할 전
망이며, 대전 사업장 또한 내년 상반기 가동 재개 예상됨에 따라 지상방산 부문은 전년 대비 
성장 폭 두드러지며 사측 가이던스 15% 매출 성장 충분히 달성 가능할 전망이다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 130 만원으로 하향 
한화에어로스페이스에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’를 유지하고, 목표주가 기존 160 만원에서 130
만원으로 하향한다. 최근 Peer 그룹의 주가 하락에 따라 멀티플 하향 조정했기 때문이다. 
폴란드 천무 유도탄 JV에 이어 현지화가 전제된 K9 3차 계약을 추진 중이며, 국내 장약 생
산능력 증설을 통한 유럽향 수요 대응과 미국 내 생산거점 구축까지 진행 중에 있다. 또한 
미국 차륜형 자주포 사업 시제 선정에 이은 궤도형 사업 수주까지 기대할 수 있다. 중동 국
가들의 방공무기체계에 대한 계약 논의는 매우 가시적인 상황이며, 사우디 MNG 사업 수주 
또한 시점의 문제일 뿐이다. 기존 유럽에 더해 중동은 현지화 전략을 기반으로 동사의 새로
운 중장기 성장동력이 될 지역이며, 올해 4 분기부터 해당 수주 모멘텀과 함께 큰 폭의 실적 
성장이 맞물리며 리레이팅 가능할 전망이다. 선제적 매수 기회라는 판단이다.

작성자(한승한)는 본 조사분석자료에 게재된 내용들이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 신의성실하게 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
본 보고서는 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공된 사실이 없습니다. 
당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다. 
종목별 투자의견은 다음과 같습니다.

Document 318: Chartpack _ Advanced economies_ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-10-06)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Chartpack _ Advanced economies_ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-10-06).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

By Advanced Economies Team
Advanced Economies:
Resilience to shock after shock
October 2026

### Page 2

• The global economy is in the midst of a growth cycle. As a result, GDP growth is more resilient to shocks than initially
anticipated.
• The figures for the second quarter bear this out, despite the sharp rise in oil prices, whose negative impact on growth is offset
by a series of reflationary forces.
• The driving force behind the current growth cycle is clear: artificial intelligence (AI).
• The world’s major economies are currently engaged in a broader push to expand their capital goods investment.

• While European and US households are experiencing more or less the same energy price shock, inflation and household
consumption remain higher in the US.
• Higher and above-target inflation calls for monetary tightening.
• Inflation is exceeding the target set by three of the four central banks examined in this publication (the US, the UK, the
Eurozone). Inflation is currently expected to peak in Q4 2026 or Q1 2027, but there is a significant risk that energy inflation will

spread further to other components of inflation in the future.
• Inflation can also be a sign of (too much) economic strength (in the US).
• Monetary policies also have to offset the inflationary effects of fiscal policy (particularly in Japan).
• The outbreak of the conflict in Iran acted as a catalyst: the ensuing rise in energy prices contributes to the recent increase in
long-term rates (which had been trending downwards at the start of the year) to levels close to those of 2002.

• The other issue is the high level of fiscal deficits (too high for a period of growth), reflecting different fiscal trajectories. In the
US, the UK, France and Germany the public debt-to-GDP ratio is increasing, while it has stabilized in Italy and decreased in
Spain and Japan.
• The rise in long-term interest rates will weigh on public finances. The fiscal arithmetic is simpler today than it will be in 2030.
By 2030, the effective rate will have risen and caught up with GDP growth.

For more information, please refer to our publication:
EcoPerspectives – Advanced Economies | 3rd quarter of 2026, as of September 24, 2026– Economic Research– BNP Paribas
Advanced economies: Resilience to shock after shock

### Page 3

3
Advanced Economies: Resilience to shock after shock (forecasts as of September 30)

Document 353: FOMC 明確さを待つために様子見

Report source: DIR
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

米国

株式会社大和総研 丸の内オフィス 〒100-6756 東京都千代田区丸の内一丁目 9 番 1 号 グラントウキョウ ノースタワー 
このレポートは投資勧誘を意図して提供するものではありません。このレポートの掲載情報は信頼できると考えられる情報源から作成しておりますが、その正確性、完全
性を保証するものではありません。また、記載された意見や予測等は作成時点のものであり今後予告なく変更されることがあります。㈱大和総研と大和証券㈱は、㈱大和
証券グループ本社を親会社とする大和証券グループの会社です。内容に関する一切の権利は㈱大和総研にあります。無断での複製・転載・転送等はご遠慮ください。

2026 年 10 月 29 日 全5 頁

FOMC 明確さを待つために様子見 
FOMC はインフレ高止まり、景気下振れ、金融面のリスクに直面

経済調査部 主任研究員 矢作 大祐 
ニューヨークリサーチセンター 研究員 藤原 翼

[要約] 
 2026 年10 月のFOMC では、FF 金利の誘導目標レンジを3.75～4.00％に据え置いた。市
場は事前にFF 金利の据え置きを織り込んでおり、決定後の長期金利、株価、ドルにも
大きな変動は見られなかった。 
 声明文では景気判断を上方修正し、ウォーシュFRB 議長も FOMC 後の記者会見で、個人
消費、設備投資、雇用環境の底堅さを強調した。そして、インフレ率は依然高すぎる
ことも引き続き強調し た。他方、金融環境に関するウォーシュ議長の指摘には変化が
見られた。資本配分の選別が進み、一部資産では信用スプレッドが急拡大するなど、
金融環境には引き締まりの兆候が表れているとした。 
 もっとも、ウォーシュ議長は金融環境の引き締まりの 程度を断定するのは時期尚早と
の見解を示した。ウォーシュ議長は今後のデータによって状況がより「明確」になる
と述べ、フォワードガイダンスの提示には慎重な姿勢を維持しつつも、12月のFOMC で
利上げに踏み切る可能性をにじませたと解釈すべきである。 
 9 月時点のFOMC 参加者の金利見通しと直近のFedWatch は、12 月のFOMC での0.25％pt
追加利上げを示唆している。しかし、実施の可否は予断を許さない。注目点は、期待
インフレ率の上振れ、雇用統計、とりわけ労働参加率の改善、社債利回りなどに表れ
る金融ストレスの 3 点である。FOMC は、利上げによる景気・金融環境の悪化と、据え
置きによるインフレ期待の上振れというジレンマに直面しており、政策判断とともに
丁寧な情報発信が求められる。

### Page 2

1

2 / 5

政策金利の据え置きを決定 
2026 年 10 月 27 日・28 日に開催された FOMC（連邦公開市場委員会）では、政策金利である
FF（フェデラルファンド）レートの誘導目標レンジを 3.75～4.00％に据え置いた。なお、今回
の決定について、クックFRB 理事とクリーブランド連銀のハマック総裁が0.25％pt の利上げを
主張して反対票を投じた。 
今回の決定は、市場にとってサプライズとはならなかった。CME が算出する FedWatch（FF 先
物市場から算出される利上げ・利下げ確率）によると、FOMC の開催直前（10 月 26 日）の時点
で、金利据え置きの確率が約 90％となっていた。前回（2026 年 9 月 15 日・16 日）の FOMC で
0.25％pt の利上げが実施された後、FOMC 参加者は根強いインフレを背景に年内の追加利上げを
主張する一方、利上げの時期に関しては慎重に評価していくとの見解を示していたことから、
市場も金利据え置きを織り込んでいた。 
なお、FOMC 後の記者会見において、ウォーシュ FRB 議長は来年以降における FOMC の開催ス
ケジュールに言及した。具体的には、今後も 1 年あたり合計 8 回の会合を行う一方で、そのう
ち 2027 年は 1 回分、2028 年は 2 回分を金融政策の広範な戦略に特化した会合に充てるとし、
金融政策の決定や声明文の公表は行わないと発表した。

声明文・記者会見：金融環境の変化を指摘する一方、慎重に評価を行う意向 
今回公表された声明文を見ると、冒頭で「委員会は10 対2 の賛成多数で以下の声明の公表を
承認した」と前置きした上 で、「委員会は、連邦準備制度の二重の目標（デュアルマンデート）
を支えるため、FF 金利の目標レンジを3.75～4.00％に維持することを決定した。委員会は、銀
行システムにおける潤沢な準備預金を維持する政策を継続する」と決定内容を示した。 
続いて、景気認識については、「経済活動は力強く拡大している。地政学の動向などにより
不確実性は依然として高いものの、国内支出は底堅く推移している。生産性の伸びは力強く、
設備投資も堅調だ」と、前回の「経済活動は堅調に拡大」から「経済活動は力強く拡大」に上
方修正された。雇用環境については、 「雇用者数の増加は労働力人口の増加に追いついており、
失業率はほとんど変化していない」との表現が据え置かれた。ウォーシュ議長は記者会見で、
2026 年上半期の実質GDP 成長率が上方修正され、第3 四半期も力強い成長が見込まれると指摘
した。さらに、個人消費は堅調で、設備投資は、けん引役となっているハイテク関連だけでは
なく、それ以外の項目にも波及が見られると述べた。他方でウォーシュ議長は、多くのFOMC 参
加者は景気が過熱しているとは評価しておらず、その要因として 、自身の見立てでは生産性が
改善していることに言及した。また、雇用環境については、新規失業保険申請件数が低水準で
推移していると指摘し、極めて良好と評価した。 
物価関連の記述を見ると、 「インフレ率は依然として高水準にある。本日の政策措置は、委
員会が掲げる 2％目標への早期回帰を支援するものである。委員会は物価安定を実現する」と

### Page 3

1

3 / 5

の表現から「インフレ率は依然として高水準にある。より多くの情報が集積されることで、委
員会はインフレ率を目標の 2％に速やかに戻すための適切な政策スタンスを決定できるだろう。
委員会は物価安定を実現する」に変更された。ウォーシュ議長は記者会見で、インフレ率は依
然として高すぎると指摘した。さらに、直近数週間においてエネルギー価格やそれに関連する
価格が再上昇していることに警戒感を示した。もっとも、ウォーシュ議長は FRB の取り組みを
評価する指標として期待インフレ率を挙げ、現時点では正しい方向に進んでいるとの認識を示
した。 
また、今回の記者会見では、金融環境についての質問が目立った。ウォーシュ議長は、一部
の企業や家計において金融環境は引き締まりつつあるとの見解を示し、 市場参加者は資本配分
について以前よりも慎重かつ選別的な姿勢をやや強めていると言及した。足元で注目される AI
関連投資の資金調達を含め、金利は幅広い分野に直接・間接の影響を及ぼしているとの認識を
示した。9 月初め以降、市場参加者が投資対象をより選別するようになり、一部の資産では信
用スプレッドが急拡大し、その他の資産でも、金融環境は大幅ではないものの、幾分引き締ま
っていると指摘した。 
もっとも、ウォーシュ議長は、金融環境は引き締まりつつあるものの、現時点で「引き締ま
った状態にある」と判断するのは時期尚早との見方を示した。その上で、今後得られるデータ
によって状況がより「明確（more clarity ）」になると述べた。 この「明確」という表現は、
様々な情報を把握するという目的で様子見を決定した今回のFOMC とは対照的な表現と捉えるべ
きだろう。とりわけ、ウォーシュ議長はフォワードガイダンスを示すことに慎重な姿勢を続け
ていることを踏まえれば、自身のそうした姿勢と矛盾しない範囲で、12 月の FOMC で利上げに
踏み切る姿勢をにじませたといえるのではないか。

FOMC はインフレ高止まり、景気下振れ、金融面のリスクに直面 
今回のFOMC では利上げをせず、様子見となった。市場でも据え置きが想定されていたことか
ら、予想通りの結果といえる。10 年債金利や主要株価指数、ドル指数などにも大きな変動は見
られなかった。 
直近のCPI、PPI が市場予想を上回るなどインフレ懸念が強まる中でも、様子見となった背景
には、景気の下振れリスクへの警戒感があるだろう。ウォーシュ議長は景気を堅調と表現した
が、経済指標を見ると、9 月の雇用統計の雇用者数や小売売上高に減速感が見られるなど、屋
台骨である個人消費には下振れリスクがある。また、けん引役の設備投資も CoreWeave 問題な
どを背景に、債務の持続可能性が警戒されており、 社債利回りは上昇している 1。金融環境全
体に関して、OFR（米財務省金融リサーチ室） の金融ストレス指数に注目すると、足元で上昇
（ストレスの強まり）傾向が見られる（図表 1 左図）。内訳を見ると、ボラティリティ要因がプ

ラスに転じ、クレジット要因のマイナス幅も縮小している。金融環境にストレスが見られつつ

では、先行きの金融政策はどう考えればよいか。9月のFOMC で公表された、FOMC 参加者のFF
金利見通し（中央値）では、2026 年12 月末時点でFF 金利は4.1％となっている。10 月のFOMC
でFF 金利が据え置かれたことから、FOMC 参加者の予想通りであれば、12 月のFOMC での0.25％
pt 追加利上げが想定される。なお、10 月 29 日時点の FedWatch でも 12 月の FOMC では 0.25％

得られるデータによって状況がより明確になると述べたことについて、利上げの可能性をにじ
ませたと解釈すべきとしたのは前述の通りだ。

他方で、実際に 12 月の FOMC で利上げを実施できるか否かは予断を許さない。利上げの可能
性を考える上では、3 つの注目点を取り上げたい。1 つ目の注目点として、インフレ率の高止ま
りリスクに関連して、期待インフレ率に注目したい。ウォーシュ議長はFOMC 後の記者会見で、
現時点で期待インフレ率が正しい方向に向かっていると評価したが、クリーブランド連銀が算
出した期待インフレ率の期間構造を見ると、2026 年 10 月は 2026 年 9 月に比べて上方シフトし
た（図表1 右図）。期待インフレ率の上方シフトがさらに進めば、アンカリング機能が低下して
いるとの懸念から、FOMC が利上げ姿勢を強めると考えられる。なお、11 月分の期待インフレ率
に関しては、11 月10 日に公表が予定されている。 
他方で、2 つ目の注目点として、実体経済の下振れリスクに関連して、11 月6 日や12 月4 日
に公表予定の雇用統計、とりわけ労働参加率が挙げられる 2。労働参加率は 8 月以降上昇に転

じた。労働需給や賃金上昇圧力の緩和だけでなく、消費者の雇用マインドの下支えという意味

FOMC は、根強いインフレ圧力に対して利上げで対処すれば景気・金融環境を悪化させかねず、
反対に据え置けばインフレ期待の上振れを招きかねないというジレンマに直面しているといえ
る。こうした状況では、政策判断そのものに加え、市場の利上げ 期待の過度な変動を招かない
よう、FOMC 参加者の丁寧なコミュニケーションも重要となろう。

Document 419: RBNZ October 2026 Monetary Policy Review (2026-10-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/RBNZ October 2026 Monetary Policy Review (2026-10-01).pdf

Westpac New Zealand Economics
Sticking with the plan
• The RBNZ left the OCR at 2.75% - a shock to
the market.
• The committee voted 4:3 against a 25bp
hike, with Governor Breman within the
“on hold” camp.
• The RBNZ delivered on its September strategy
of pausing to assess the impact of OCR
increases done to date.
• The MPC remains of the view that the OCR will
increase eventually, although views on the
timing differ among members.
• Westpac expects a 25bp hike in December and

continues to expect an eventual peak OCR of
4% in September next year.
• However, a December hike is not a done
deal and will depend on data on fuel prices,
inflation expectations and second-round
pricing pressures and information on evolving
global economic risks that accumulate in the
next six weeks.

RBNZ September 2026 MPS

Westpac forecast

Sources: Bloomberg, RBNZ, Westpac
Forecasts
OCR remains at 2.75%, further hikes remain
their base case.
Today the RBNZ left the OCR unchanged at 2.75%. This
was a shock to markets and most analysts as a 25bp hike
had been nearly fully priced by markets. The decision
went to a vote which resulted in a 4:3 split in favour of an
unchanged OCR.
The RBNZ has followed through on the pause it signalled
for this meeting back in the September Monetary Policy

Statement. All MPC members continue to see a case for a
higher OCR in time, but there are different views around
the timing of those increases. The Governor noted that
further interest rate increases remain likely in coming
meetings but are conditional on the whether the outlook
evolves in line with expectations.
The key case made by more hawkish MPC members
(Silk, Gourley and Conway) put greater weight on the
persistence of underlying inflation and the risk that higher

inflation becomes entrenched in price-setting behaviour.
They noted that core inflation has been above levels
consistent with the 2% target midpoint for some time,
28 Oct 2026 | Kelly Eckhold, Chief Economist | +64 9 348 9382 | +64 21 786 758 | kelly.eckhold@westpac.co.nz
ECONOMIC BULLETIN
RBNZ October 2026 Monetary Policy Review.

### Page 2

2
Westpac New Zealand Economics
and viewed aggregate economic growth as relatively
resilient despite weakness among many households and
businesses. In their view, tightening now would guard
against sustained inflation pressures and reduce the risk
of needing to do more later.
The case made by the majority in favour of no change
in the OCR (Breman, Hansen, Gai and Williams) argued
that the two recent OCR increases warranted more time
to assess their effects. They placed weight on inflation

having been weaker than expected in the Q3 data (the
Governor emphasised this point in answer to questions
at the press conference), smaller-than-anticipated
indirect effects from higher fuel prices, stable inflation
expectations and subdued wage growth. They also
emphasised downside risks to the recovery, particularly
the continued weakness in household consumption and
investment, and judged that there was little evidence
so far that businesses were using higher costs as an

opportunity to rebuild margins.
One point of note today is that the RBNZ seems
more concerned about risks to the global outlook,
notwithstanding its recent resilience – possibly
channelling some recent comments made by the IMF. The
RBNZ notes that geopolitical events, high energy prices,
and high and rising public and private debt levels pose
downside risk to global growth. It also notes uncertainty
about whether the surge in AI investment will be justified

by economic fundamentals. In addition, the MPC debated
whether higher global interest rates reflected a higher
neutral rate – due to higher inflation expectations and
demand for funding – or posed the risk of bringing
forward a potential global downturn (possibly leading to a
rapid decline in the event of an abrupt global adjustment).
The exchange rate has adjusted sharply lower today,
reflecting the market shock. The NZD/AUD exchange rate
has moved to a new cycle low of around 0.7980 at the

time of writing. This is in line with our year-end target for
the NZD/AUD exchange rate of 0.79 cents. Compounding
the downward pressure on the NZD/AUD exchange rate
has been strong CPI data in Australia combined with
hawkish RBA commentary. Our colleagues in Australia
continue to expect a Cash Rate hike in Australia on
Melbourne Cup Day and some risk of a further Cash
Rate rise in February. Hence downward pressure on the
NZD exchange rate seems a reasonable prospect given

interest rate differentials are weighing heavily on the NZD.
The RBNZ does not sound especially perturbed by the
prospect of weakness in the exchange rate and noted
that the low exchange rate is supportive of the medium-
term prospects of the export sector and the economy
more broadly.
Westpac outlook – December hike likely, but
not done deal.
We changed our forecasts from no change in the OCR
at the October meeting to a 25bp hike in the wake of the

CPI data last week. Given the absence of the hike today
we are reverting to the OCR forecast profile we had had
in place since July – i.e. one further 25bp hike in 2026
and then 25bp increases in the February and March
2027 meetings, followed by a slower pace of increases
at the June and September Monetary Policy Statement
meetings. Our forecast of a peak OCR of 4% from
September 2027 has remained unchanged throughout, as
has our assessment of a 3.75% neutral OCR.

That said, in the current uncertain environment, it goes
without saying that the evolution of monetary policy will
depend on how both global events and key data evolve
(we summarise the near-term economic diary below).
Therefore, as the RBNZ noted itself today, the exact timing
of the tightening profile is highly uncertain and even the
tightening we forecast at the December meeting should
not be regarded as a done deal.
Things to watch ahead of the 9 December

meeting.
The RBNZ’s next policy review is on 9 December, when it
will also publish a full Monetary Policy Statement (MPS)
and refreshed economic forecasts. How the RBNZ’s policy
stance evolves between now and then will depend on
both international and domestic factors.
Regarding the former, the MPC has clearly become
more concerned about risks to the global economic
outlook, stemming from geopolitical events, high energy
prices, high and rising public and private debt levels and

uncertainty about whether the surge in AI investment will
be justified by economic fundamentals. So, the RBNZ will
pay close attention to any developments that suggest
that downside risk to the global outlook is materialising.
There are only a few important domestic economic
indicators scheduled for release ahead of the 9 December
meeting (by contrast there is a full slate of indicators due
for release prior to the following meeting on 10 February).

We think the following are the key ones to watch.
• Q3 labour market surveys
(4 November): Indicators from these surveys will cast
light on economic activity in Q3, capacity pressures
in the labour market and whether the lift in headline
inflation is impacting growth in wages and salaries.
• Q4 RBNZ surveys
(17 November, 20 November, 24 November): Inflation
expectations measures from the RBNZ’s surveys of
professional forecasters, households and businesses

will be analysed to assess the impact of elevated
headline inflation.

### Page 3

3
Westpac New Zealand Economics
• October Selected Prices
(17 November): These pricing indicators will provide a
partial read on how inflation is tracking early in Q4.
• October/November ANZ Business Outlook
(29 October/30 November): The ANZ surveys will
provide a timely account of how business activity and
pricing behaviour is responding to the sharp rebound
in oil prices.
• October PMI and PSI surveys
(13 November/16 November): These may provide some

early insight regarding GDP growth in Q4.
In addition to the above, we will monitor a range of
other high-frequency indicators, such as monthly data
on filled jobs, consumer spending, building consents,
housing market activity and prices, job ads and consumer
confidence. We will also be paying close attention
to developments in prices for New Zealand’s key
export commodities.
Finally, with the General Election taking place on
7 November, it is possible that opinions regarding the

likely path of monetary policy could be impacted should
a Labour Party-led government – with an intention
to restore the RBNZ’s dual mandate – seem likely to
be formed.

Document 430: Swing State_ Navigating Midterm Volatility (2026-10-23)

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank

Swing State: Navigating 
Midterm Volatility

1

Laura Daniels

Josh Ray

Matthew Barnard

Parag Thatte 
Strategist

Brett Ryan 
Senior US Economist

October 23, 2026 
A client-facing version of this report is available at the Deutsche Bank Research 
Portal. The following Deutsche Bank Research analysts contributed to this report: 
Brett Ryan (econ), Binky Chadha and Parag Thatte (equity strategy), Benjamin 
Soff (data centers), Daniel Knauff (software), George Hill (healthcare), Omotayo 
Okusanya (REITs), Peter Abramowitz (office REITs), Mark DeVries (consumer and 
specialty finance), Nicole DeBlase (MI/EE), Corinne Blanchard (clean tech), 
Richa Harnain (transports), and Scott Deuschle (aerospace and defense).  
With the 2026 US midterm elections approaching, we look at key investor 
debates surrounding the event and legislative priorities of the next Congress. 
Despite the uncertainty that midterms can bring, the market appears fairly 
confident on expectations for the overall results. While a surprise Republican-
majority victory in both chambers is still possible, consensus is converging 
around current polling indicating Democrats are likely to flip the seats needed for 
a House majority and may win a Senate majority. Historically, an incumbent 
president has lost seats in 20 of the last 22 midterms. Investor expectations are 
in line with this. Polymarket, for example, is currently above 90% for Democrats 
gaining control of the House and above 60% for Democrats gaining control of the 
Senate.  
With this set of expectations, key investor debates instead focus on the 
legislative agenda for hot-ticket policy issues. Namely, these hinge on AI and 
data centers, affordability (affecting healthcare, housing, and education loans), 
and economic and fiscal policy. Debates also surround possible influence of the 
new Congress on the war in Iran and, by extension, energy prices. In this report, 
we cut through the noise on what the next Congress could reasonably advance 
from these agendas, what the impact would be on affected sectors, and how to 
address market uncertainty – and perceived certainty – into the elections. 
Historically, midterm elections have proven to be a check on administration 
policies. The number of seats lost in the midterms by the incumbent party is tied 
to the president’s approval rating going into midterms, which in turn tends to 
align with consumer confidence. President Trump’s ~38% approval rating, at a 
record low for his tenure, would suggest a little over a 45-seat loss for the 
Republican Party (in the House and Senate combined). That said, polling in the 
Senate races that will determine control of the chamber largely remains within 
the margin of error, and in 2024, Trump won in at least five of the seven core toss-
up states. Thus, the Senate remains a much closer call than the House race.

### Page 2

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 2

Figure 1: Presidential Approval vs. House Seats

Source: Real Clear Politics

Figure 2: Presidential Approval vs. Consumer Confidence

Source: The Conference Board

Whatever the outcome of the  midterms, the legislative agenda will 
still face constraints from the White House. It is all but impossible for either party 
to gain a veto -proof majority  in either chamber, minimizing the likelihood of a 
large shift in fiscal or regulatory policy. The most  immediately obvious impact of 
Democrats taking the Senate would be the ability to slow or block executive 
branch appointees, including at the Federal Reserve Board of Governors or the 
Supreme Court, should any members step down before their terms end.

### Page 3

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 3

Without garnering a two-thirds majority in both chambers, Democrats lack a clear 
path to unwind the 2025 tax cuts or enact AI-related regulatory legislation – at 
least, not without President Trump’s explicit approval. With respect to the near -
term fiscal policy outlook, the most important period for investors may be the 
“lame duck” session, the period after the November 3 election and before the new 
Congress convenes on January 3. Recall that current federal government funding 
expires on December 11, and the Trump administration is asking for a nearly 60% 
increase in defense spending, the largest since the Korean War.  In addition, the 
debt limit will become binding again around the middle of next year. In short, the 
actions during the lame duck session could determine whether the new Congress 
is seated amid a government shutdown, a contentious battle over the federal 
budget, and the debt limit looming in the background.  
In terms of the market reaction to midterm  elections, the risk-reward profile has 
typically been favorable , with 21 of the last 23 midterm years  seeing positive 
returns in the fourth quarter, with an average gain of 7% in the S&P. The gains are 
notable even after adjusting for typical positive year-end seasonality.

Figure 3: S&P Returns and Midterms

Source: Bloomberg Finance LP

It w ould not be unusual for the market to go sideways as investors hedge 
potential volatility and uncertainty, as we have seen previously ahead of other 
well-telegraphed pivotal events. This has historically been followed by a rally as 
the results became clear and  uncertainty dissipated, regardless of the result 
itself. In the current context, if election polls and prediction market probabilities 
continue being lopsided, the market may not treat the election as an uncertain 
risk event going in. Uncertainty could still ramp up after, and the typical midterm 
election rally may be pushed out. It is notable that the one prominent exception 
to the midterm rallies was in 2018 , when the market sold off sharply after the 
elections as uncertainty spiked around trade war 1.0 as well as Fed policy.

### Page 4

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 4

We note that the midterm elections also coincide with the third quarter earnings 
season, during which we expect companies to deliver growth of 34%. For next 
year, we expect growth to be a still -robust 17% with risks to the upside. 
Eventually, earnings tend to be the anchor for equities and have been for several 
years despite numerous domestic political as well as geopolitical shocks. 
Across sectors, most have rallied around past midterms in keeping with the 
market, but there isn’t a consistent pattern of out - or underperformance . This 
suggests that for the initial conditions for each sector, specific policies and 
positioning played a bigger role.  For that reason, we turn to a closer analysis of 
key legislative and policy debates impacting exposed sectors.

### Page 5

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 5

AI and Data Centers  
AI and data centers have become a campaign talking point for candidates from 
both the Democratic and Republican parties. This has spurred investor questions 
on whether to expect a national regulatory framework constraining data center 
buildouts. Some of th e headlines surrounding this debate overestimate what 
Congress can  accomplish through national legislation compared with what is 
possible at a state level, particularly during a presidential administration 
supportive of AI and data center acceleration. Still, the industry is responding to 
a political backdrop that is increasingly scrutinizing AI and data centers.  
The current administration has prioritized advancing AI and digital infrastructure 
development. The White House ’s AI Action Plan , released July 2025, included a 
set of executive orders supporting the rapid buildout of AI data centers by 
reducing certain federal regulatory and permitting burdens. The administration 
did not preempt state laws on data center infrastructure development and 
federal permitting orders generally do not override state zoning and utility rules. 
In March 2026 , the White House announced the Ratepayer Protection Pledge, 
under which large data center operators would voluntarily commit to covering 
the cost to upgrade generation, transmission, substations, or other necessary 
infrastructure to service these developments.  The industry’s response to these 
proposals has been largely constructive. The Data Center Coalition  (DCC), an 
industry group representing major  hyperscalers and data center  owners and 
operators came out in support of the initial version of the Pledge, while arguing 
that large -load customers across all industries also should cover the costs to 
upgrade energy infrastructure. According to the DCC , “the data center 
industry remains committed to being responsible partners where we  operate, 
which includes  paying our full energy costs  and investing in local jobs and 
workforce development.” 
Congress voted in September 2026 to codify the principles present in the Pledge 
via the Ratepayer Protection Act. The bill, which only required states to “consider” 
these standards, passed overwhelmingly in the House (417-3), but failed to reach 
60 votes in the Senate (57 -43). Critics noted the bill stopped short of imposing 
mandatory nationwide rules. 
Looking forward, we expect a Congress that is more focused on consumer 
protection might look to codify a more stringent version of these rules, requiring 
large-load customers to bear the full incremental costs associated with 
generation, transmission, and distribution upgrades.  However, even if such a 
framework were passed into law, we believe the overall economic impact to large 
data center operators would be relatively modest, for two reasons. 
For one, the White House would maintain a veto over  bills that include language 
it views as overly burdensome towards AI infrastructure development . Two, data 
center operators  generally underwrite projects only when they have a high 
degree of confidence in securing power and, where necessary, may already be 
embedding the cost of upgrading energy infrastructure into their investment 
plans. We expect developers to be capable of passing on sustained increases in 
power infrastructure costs to their customers , which, for many 100  MW+ 
developments, are the major investment-grade hyperscalers.

### Page 6

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 6

Though data center development is receiving increasing national attention, it is 
still largely a local issue, and the most consequential elections will be at the state 
and local levels. We have seen a number of state -driven actions on data center s 
in recent months, including: 
• In July, New York State’s governor issued an executive order to establish 
a temporary moratorium on data center development  while the state 
develops a standardized framework of rules for key issues (including 
responsibilities for power and energy infrastructure upgrades; additional 
power generation; clean energy; water use, emission, and waste 
management; and state/local financ ial incentives). The New York 
legislature also passed a one -year moratorium on Department of 
Environmental Conservation permits and approvals for 20  MW+ data 
center developments. 
• In August, Texas’ governor directed the state’s public utility commission 
to conduct a comprehensive audit of every data center advancing 
through the ERCOT (Texas’s grid operator) interconnection process. The 
audit will cover several notable areas including public financial support, 
electricity demand and supply, water use, community impacts, and 
ownership and control. 
• In September, California’s governor passed a comprehensive regulatory 
package, covering electricity, water, reporting, and environmental 
review. Most of these provisions take effect January 1, 2027, and phase 
in through 2027 -2028, but the package does not ban data centers or 
impose a statewide moratorium on development activity. 
• Oregon, Pennsylvania, New Jersey, and other states have also 
established orders to create standards and frameworks governing data 
center development (but without imposing broad moratoriums). 
At the more local level, a large number of moratoriums have been passed on new 
data center development. Data collected by SemiAnalysis, an independent 
research firm, shows that, as of September, more than 300 local data  center 
moratoriums have been adopted nationwide across counties, municipalities, 
townships, and other local governments.  
However, these state and local moratoriums appear  thus far to be less impactful 
to near- and medium-term development activity than headline coverage might 
otherwise suggest. According to SemiAnalysis, only about 2.3 GW of the 38 GW 
of data center capacity planned or under construction for 2027 has faced delays 
as a result of  the New York state executive order (0.8 GW) and/or local 
moratoriums (1.5 GW). 
We believe the primary reasons that moratoriums are, in reality, less likely to 
impact near -term development activity are (1) that these moratoriums largely 
impact new projects and applications (rather than existing developments in  
progress), (2) there may be flexibility to relocate or rezone early-stage projects in 
response to moratoriums, and (3) data center developers are increasingly 
partnering with local communities to gain their support via investments and other 
programs (as we wrote about recently).  Potential regulatory risk has not yet 
slowed capex spending patterns, with capex spend still expected to climb from

### Page 7

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 7

$0.5 trillion in 2025 to $1.3 trillion by 2027; data centers typically represent ~20-
30% of the total bill of materials for new AI capacity.  
Although the practical impact on near- and medium-term development pipelines 
appears less pronounced than headlines might suggest, all this is not to say that 
data centers will not face increasing local opposition following the midterms. 
Indeed, i n our conversations with industry participants, there is an 
acknowledgment that greater public scrutiny of data centers is not going away.  
Additional moratoriums, zoning restrictions, permitting requirements, or utility -
related conditions could affect the pace, cost , and overall feasibility of future 
projects.

### Page 8

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 8

Affordability Agenda: Healthcare, Housing, and 
Education Loans  
The affordability agenda has become a centerpiece of many Democratic as well 
as Republican campaign platforms.  This platform is impacting investor 
debates across healthcare, housing, and student loans. In healthcare,  the push 
among some Democrats for expanded health coverage under the Medicare for All 
banner is placing pressure on managed care stocks, but meanwhile, companies 
with exposure to Medicaid and  the Affordable Care Act (ACA) could see a 
supportive political backdrop. On housing, investor debates on the impacts of the 
affordability agenda have continued since passage of legislation in July 2026 that 
caps the number of single -family homes for -profit institutional investors can 
purchase. Affordability of higher education is likewise part of the conversatio n, 
leading investors to debate which levers Congress could pull that would affect 
student loan companies.  
Healthcare 
In healthcare, w e see the upcoming midterm elections as a sentiment event for 
managed care stocks , not a policy event.  With the presidential veto in place 
through 2028, we do not expect the government to enact direct healthcare 
legislation during the next Congress. Forecasts place the probability of a change 
in House control at roughly 79%, with both chambers changing in about 44% of 
simulations. Although investor skittishness around Medicare for All (M4A) rhetoric 
is high, we see the likelihood of M4A replacing private coverage as a near -zero 
probability in this timeframe.  
M4A has no realistic legislative path before 2029, but the rhetoric weighs on 
managed care organization (MCO) stock multiples by raising perceived long-term 
risk rather than affecting near -term earnings. Polling from  Kaiser Family 
Foundation, a health policy research organization, shows support for M4A at a 
decade-plus high, and the 2018 –2019 cycle suggests that increased campaign 
attention can pressure MCO multiples even without a direct policy impact. We 
expect similar sentiment -driven pressure through year -end and potentially into 
the 2028 primary cycle but would not view it as a change i n near -term 
fundamentals.  
For Medicare Advantage, we see a h eadline risk over earnings reality. The near-
term earnings impact for affected companies should be limited, because the US 
Centers for Medicare & Medicaid Services (CMS) has finalized a 2.48% Medicare 
Advantage (MA) rate increase for 2027, versus 0.09% in the advance notice. A 
change in House control would instead increase focus on 2028 payment policy, 
risk adj ustment, and prior authorization. Coding reform already has bipartisan 
support: sponsors estimate that the Cassidy -Merkley No UPCODE Act could 
generate up to $275  billion of savings over ten years, while MedPAC estimates 
coding intensity will add roughly $40  billion to 2025 Medicare Advantage  costs 
relative to traditional Medicare. In our view, the more immediate consideration is 
potential multiple pressure rather than a measurable change to company 
earnings.  
However, the next Congress could b enefit Medicaid funding and ACA policy. 
Medicaid and the ACA appear relatively better positioned, although any benefit

### Page 9

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 9

would likely be reflected first in sentiment and lower perceived policy risk rather 
than near -term estimate revisions. Medicaid community engagement 
requirements take effect in most states by January 1, 2027. Although repeal 
remains unlikely given the presidential veto, a change in House control could shift 
the year -end funding debate toward  restoring or  preserving federal Medicaid 
support, which could ease state budget pressure and support rate -setting. The 
ACA policy backdrop could also become more supportive, although we see 
limited scope for enhanced premium subsidies to return before 2029 after the 
three-year extension passed by the House stalled.  
Housing 
Turning to the housing sector, the likely midterm outcome of a split government 
would result in more gridlock. Historically, political gridlock in terms of passing 
new regulation has been a tailwind for the real estate sector, reducing regulatory 
risk at the federal level. In the scenario of a split Congress, in which the Democrats 
have a majority in the House but not the Senate, bills with bipartisan support have 
a greater likelihood to become law. Two key areas in which this bipartisan support 
exists are housing affordability and consumer protection. 
Among real estate investment trusts ( REITs), we see Residential as among the 
most impacted subsectors, particularly as it relates to the affordability debate. 
Regardless of the election outcome, housing affordability has become front and 
center on the political agenda  across both parties. F ederal legislation  is more 
likely to target supply -side solutions, as legislation restricting rent increases is 
typically handled at the local and state level. We think Multifamily would be the 
most likely subsector impacted, as a push to expand rental housing supply could 
lead to eased zoning restrictions, particularly in supply -constrained coastal 
markets that have historically benefited from higher barriers to entry. That said, 
this would likely take years to materialize. With a Democratic majority , Congress 
could voice opposition to the influence of large institutional landlords, but we see 
this strictly as a sentiment risk to stocks.  
Single Family For Rent (SFR) is less exposed to near -term legislative shocks, as 
the sector was a focus of the 21st Century ROAD to Housing Act passed in July 
2026 over the summer. The  measure impacts institutional owners’ ability to buy 
homes off the MLS but contains specific carve-out provisions that will allow them 
to keep developing build -to-rent (BTR) product and buy excess inventory from 
homebuilders, the sector's preferred acquis ition channel in recent years. The 
ROAD to Housing Act also contained provisions that could theoretically ease the 
production of manufactured homes, the main business segment for 
Manufactured Housing (MH) REITs. The first-order impact is likely more flexibility 
in home designs that could lead to further densification of MH communities, 
although we note emerging investor debate about the long -term impacts of 
increased MH production on competitive supply for a property type t hat has 
historically been supply constrained, mainly due to NIMBY-ism.  
Parallel to the conversation on affordability, it is clear that, regardless of which 
political party may be in power following the midterms, a major focus remains 
addressing the US fiscal deficit and its continued growth. We continue to pay 
attention to potential budget cuts or sequestration to slow government spending 
and reduce the deficit, which could have an impact on real estate.  Key areas to 
watch include cuts to healthcare spending (which would be a negative for skilled

### Page 10

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 10

nursing focused REITs, given Medicare is a major revenue source for their tenant 
base) and cuts that would impact Office REITs with large government exposure.  
We also worry that an elevated federal deficit and the elevated Treasury 
borrowing needs today create a risk that interest rates will remain  higher for 
longer. This puts pressure on real estate valuations and cost of financing for REITs 
to drive external growth via acquisitions and development. A split Congress is 
likely to result in more difficult budget negotiations and increased oversight, but 
it remains unclear w hat the ultimate outcome would be to address the budget 
deficit issue. 
With real estate being more dependent on local market dynamics, we believe the 
more likely regulatory impact would come from local regulation. This could show 
up on the ballots during the midterms , with increased possibility of rent control 
legislation targeted at residential real estate as well as self -storage, as cities and 
municipalities focus on affordability issues. There is also growing legislative 
pushback at the local level against development of real assets such as data 
centers given the large power requirements, as discussed in detail elsewhere in 
this report.  
Education Lending 
Midterm campaign platforms on education affordability  have a bearing on the 
consumer and specialty finance sector. Much of the policy exposure in this sector 
is through the executive branch bodies, not Congress, however. This includes, for 
example, regulatory authorities  such as the FDIC (Federal Deposit Insurance 
Corporation), the CFPB (Consumer Financial Protection Bureau ), or the FHFA 
(Federal Housing Finance Agency, which regulates Fannie Mae and Freddie 
Mac). That said, historical data shows that student loan company stocks can still 
directionally align with midterm party outcome and expectations  in Congress, 
although the sample size is small.

### Page 11

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 11

Mixed Impacts of Economic and Fiscal Policy  
Democratic and Republican candidates have aimed to distinguish themselves 
from their opponents along a range of economic and fiscal policy matters, 
including tariffs, tax, labor, and the budget. In reality, the outcome of the 
midterms, regardless of which party prevails, is likely to present a mixture of relief 
and restrictions affecting businesses. In some cases, investor fears over sweeping 
economic policy changes are overblown for topics over which Congress 
realistically has limited influence, presenting more headline  risk than a risk to 
fundamentals. In considering these, we look to industrials that are particularly 
sensitive to key investor debates , spanning multi-industry/electrical equipment, 
clean tech and alternative energy, transport, and defense.  
For the multi-industry and electrical equipment (MI/EE) sector, historical midterm 
election cycles suggest a generally constructive backdrop heading into election 
day, with positive returns in six of the past seven elections. On average, the MI/EE 
group has gained ~5% during the three months preceding historical midterm 
elections and ~8% during the final month prior. Relative outperformance v ersus 
the S&P 500 has been less consistent; we observed outperformance in only four 
of seven cycles, though the group still generated average excess returns of 
~250bps over the preceding three months and ~270bps over the preceding 
month.  
Overall, the data supports a favorable absolute return backdrop ahead of 
midterms, but significant stock -level dispersion suggests company -specific 
fundamentals and factor leadership have historically mattered more than the 
election cycle alone. We mapped out election results scenarios across key policy 
pillars for our coverage (tax policy and capital investment; trade, tariffs, and 
supply chains; energy, grid, and power infrastructure; AI and data center 
regulation; and the regulatory environment). We focus on two key scenarios: one 
in which Republicans maintain control of both chambers, and a divided 
government that sees the Democrats controlling one or both chambers of the 
legislative branch.  
Under a divided government, we expect stock -level dispersion would likely 
narrow, with globally exposed companies receiving some trade policy relief and 
HVAC/automation companies benefiting from a potentially more supportive 
regulatory backdrop. However, should Republicans maintain control of Congress, 
our scenario analysis suggests this would be favorable for electrical equipment 
and power infrastructure manufacturers, due to a combination of pro-investment 
tax policy, faster infrastructure deployment, and a lighter AI regulatory 
framework. That said, globally exposed companies could face incremental tariff 
and retaliation risks.  
Looking to the clean tech and alternative energy sector, the midterm elections 
could represent a positive tailwind . T he next Congress might be positioned to 
pursue targeted extensions or modifications to certain clean -energy tax credits. 
The introduction of H.R. 8477, which proposes to restore several clean energy tax 
credits rolled back in late 2025, seems to signal tha t some, albeit a limited 
number, of Republicans could be supportive of this initiative. If the Democrats 
gain a majority in the House but not the Senate in the midterms, it is plausible that 
Democrats could find sufficient  Republican support to advance

### Page 12

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 12

legislation extending clean -energy credits, though they would still face 
significant legislative hurdles. A midterm outcome in which Republicans retain 
control of both chambers is likely to maintain the status quo  for the remaining 
existing tax credits, especially for those tied to domestic manufacturing. While 
those could potentially see further extension, we think this is a limited outcome.    
While we see a low probability of a major change in, or support for, the residential 
tax credits that expired at the end of 2025 (25D), we envision a scenario in which 
indirect residential tax credits (48E) could be extended, which would help boost 
part of the demand. Softness  in demand is expected to remain going into 2027, 
still impacted by the phaseout of the 25D tax credits, the eventual roll-off of safe-
harbor benefits, and a more constrained financing environment.  
From a manufacturing tax credit standpoint,  there could be additional tailwinds  
if we were to see any manufacturing tax credit extension or positive modification 
(although lower probability, in our view) . This would also likely improve utility -
scale project economics if broader project -related credits are preserved, though 
upcoming elections could cause a slowdown in new booking activity until 
legislative uncertainty clears.  
For Transports,  the midterms would be most relevant for the trucking supply 
thesis. Truckload stocks could have a modest negative reaction to a Democratic 
majority if investors expect some rollback of the Trump administration’s 
constraints on drivers , which has tightened supply . We would separate 
perception from fundamentals, however. The election of a Democratic Congress 
would not automatically by itself change leadership at the Department of 
Transportation (DOT) and the Federal Motor Carrier Safety Administration 
(FMCSA), the executive branch entities driving much of the current push  for 
restrictions on drivers.  
One possible impact of  the midterms for  Transports is if  a change in 
Congressional control  contributed to a faster resolution of the war in Iran . Any 
resulting lower energy prices would be a positive for Transports . We view this as 
a lower -probability outcome,  however, given the constraints on a divided 
government.  
With clashes over the budget likely looming, aerospace and def ense could get 
caught in the crosshairs. W hile defense spending has historically enjoyed 
bipartisan support, it has grown politicized following the war in Iran, making it 
more difficult for a split Congress to pass such a budget. The defense budget has 
experienced significant growth over the last few years, rising from $900 billion in 
FY2024 to $1 trillion in FY2026. The Trump administration would like to see this 
trend accelerate: in April 2026, the president issued a budget request that called 
for a $1.5  trillion budget for the Department of Defense in FY 2027. With a 
continuing resolution likely into year-end, the outcome of the midterm elections 
will determine whether the next Congress can pass such a budget. If Republicans 
hold the House and Senate, we think there  is a reasonable prospect of a budget 
of $1.2 trillion or more  passing into law . Defense investors would see that as a 
substantial tailwind for beaten-up defense stocks. On the other hand, this would 
be unlikely in a split Congress, in our view.  Given all this, we see the outcome of 
the upcoming midterm elections as key to determining how US defense stocks 
will perform into year end and beyond.

### Page 13

23 October 2026 
The Theme Screen

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 13

Taking Stock of the Known Unknowns 
Although Republicans could maintain control of both chambers,  consensus is 
converging around polls pointing to a Democratic majority in the House  and 
possibly the Senate. The perceived certainty has allowed the investor debate 
related to midterms to focus not only on who will win but how they will govern. In 
some cases, headlines are misrepresenting what a Congress constrained by the 
executive will be able to accomplish . In other areas, legislation that is expected 
to be passed will need to be taken together with offsetting factors. Whatever the 
outcome, the political backdrop will continue to influence the market and 
investor debates in the year ahead.
